# 2026-11-19 15:27 UTC — target-aware generic search scoring fixed

- **Third concrete policy defect:** generic scoreSearchCard returned unconditional high scores for Faba, Guzma, Plumeria, Team Flare Grunt, Team Rocket's Handiwork and Eri before reaching later target-aware branches. Those later branches were unreachable. In Teammates / generic searches, the policy therefore selected dead Supporters even when the opponent had no Tool, Stadium, Special Energy, Energy or visible Item target.
- **Fix:** moved the intended target-aware conditions into the first reachable branches and removed their unreachable duplicates:
  - Faba: opponent Tool, Special Energy or any Stadium required.
  - Guzma: prefer only when own Active is not already Snorlax and a Snorlax is available to promote.
  - Plumeria: opponent has Energy and own hand has at least three cards to pay its discard cost.
  - Team Flare Grunt: opponent Active has Energy.
  - Team Rocket's Handiwork: opponent deck is at or below 15.
  - Eri: an opponent Item is actually visible.
- **Regression:** extended the Teammates test with a positive Tool-only / no-Stadium case (Tool Jammer + Faba) and a negative no-target case (Tool Jammer + Team Rocket's Handiwork). The negative fixture explicitly fills the board with the candidate's other Pokémon and attaches Forest Seal Stone so those basic-search alternatives do not distort the test. Exact rank-3 printings matter: Team Flare Grunt XY 129 and Team Rocket's Handiwork FCO 112; earlier wrong-printing draft (FLF 73 / Handiwork GRI 124) was rejected by the actual policy and corrected before the final run.
- **Validation:** all **49/49** test functions passed in the deterministic V8 harness using the actual policy source and Agent49 request/decision validators. Teammates positive request digest `cba805216ec3a03685219d88b5d108efa6dcc80527eb0db48c6ad0475d3410d3`; no-target digest `3b1df3f28c562b21507fe93515c68f76644c1bb89be711f6217b910ad4e77899`. This remains synthetic evidence only, not node --test, worker IPC, Twinleaf, or native game evidence.
- **Current source bindings:** policy commit `17811b8e3f7c0464e0b9afefb56b3b5516c38fa3`, blob `69b13c1ff1304f39e7c3aa6000c97cb468f161cb`, SHA-256 `675bf05be67f71d6556341c33bb0a2cb38f6a7107be33b8e782147689cacf0f4`; test commit `0c3cc7396dfe7a0cdb92dcfa1500fa1a034a73a1`, blob `1e531b234aaa9b4a296d4d69d5f992113d5363c1`, SHA-256 `9d624c29bcee8324a703ebe93ab9be5d661443ebbc4ed767ce5ad73f07af3669`.

# 2026-11-19 15:20 UTC — Get Lost recovery-target defect fixed

- **Second concrete policy defect:** the fixed candidate's Get Lost target ranking treated spent VS Seeker / Night Stretcher Items as recoverable and did not recognize the candidate's Arven SVI 166 as a VS Seeker-recoverable Supporter. This made the policy prioritize dead Items and miss the candidate's Arven Tool-removal escape line.
- **Rule / source reasoning:** VS Seeker retrieves a Supporter from discard; it cannot retrieve itself or Night Stretcher. Night Stretcher retrieves Pokémon or Basic Energy, not Items or Supporters. Candidate deck `candidate-hood-shadow-rider.json` contains VS Seeker ×2, Arven SVI 166 ×1, Acerola BUS 112 ×1, Guzma BUS 115 ×1, Raihan EVS 152 ×1, Teammates PRC 141 ×1, Pokémon Ranger STS 104 ×1, Iono PAL 185 ×1, and Lillie's Determination MEG 119 ×2.
- **Fix:** introduced exact-candidate recoverable-Supporter priorities; Get Lost root scoring now recognizes those Supporters in the opponent discard, and Get Lost prompt scoring ranks them above spent Items. Priority order begins Arven, Guzma, Acerola, Raihan, Teammates, Pokémon Ranger, Iono, Lillie's Determination. The no-target prompt now selects the legal empty-selection token when only spent Items remain.
- **Regression:** extended the existing Get Lost test with direct score assertions (Arven alone → 150; spent VS Seeker/Night Stretcher only → 65), a positive prompt selecting Arven + Acerola, and a negative prompt selecting empty. Re-ran all **49/49** test functions in the deterministic V8 harness, with the actual policy module and Agent49 worker validators. The run is not node --test, worker IPC, Twinleaf, or native game evidence.
- **Current source bindings:** policy commit `c73245b6a4ea48e03db08ebdaed1aeeb600b63eb`, blob `18ba4a18cfe69bc6059d0d3941c9aa1dfea3ed12`, SHA-256 `8feb6c6d28e04c73ff7d2ff75b315c955a8bf0073228451184d97a6388320509`; test commit `47170237ed7a6dc3463c11bae74d0aa6a27be9a6`, blob `2468511c334357c0c653291f5f318071a0549a21`, SHA-256 `96a4ad2ad6452b6494cce0dddf9906f6e861ceb25048fe22e196278e775b398a`.

- **Focused review:** Agent42 proposed replacing the existing exponential mixture components in `scripts/aichi-correlated-round-evidence.cjs` with their convex-chord e-factors. The derivation is correct under the unchanged conditional null (E[Y_t\mid\mathcal F_{t-1}]\le q), with (Y_t=\mathrm{wins}_t/64\in[0,1]). Each finite factor (B_\lambda(y)=[1+y(e^\lambda-1)]/[1-q+qe^\lambda]) is nonnegative, has conditional expectation at most one, and pointwise dominates the old (A_\lambda(y)=e^{\lambda y}/[1-q+qe^\lambda]). The limit factor (y/q) is valid and dominates the old all-perfect indicator component. With the same weights this is a pathwise no-smaller mathematical e-process under the same assumptions.
- **Independent numerical reproduction:** with current (q=0.995), 95% primary-component weight, existing ten lambdas, and a deterministic path of 63/64 wins every 100th 64-game round and otherwise perfect: old crossing 1,743 rounds; chord crossing 622; all-perfect crossing 603 for both. This is only a synthetic calculation, not game evidence.
- **Concrete implementation correction sent to Agent42:** `log1p(eta*(y-q))) is algebraically correct but numerically unstable at the existing (lambda=32), (q=.995), (y=0) endpoint. In Node 22 it computes (1.2656542480726785e-14) instead of (1.2727804571953948e-14), a relative error of -0.56%, violating numerical pathwise dominance in that endpoint (conservatively). Recommend the equivalent stable form (B_\lambda(y)=[y+(1-y)e^{-\lambda}]/[q+(1-q)e^{-\lambda}]), with log factor `Math.log(y+(1-y)*Math.exp(-lambda))-Math.log(q+(1-q)*Math.exp(-lambda))`. Checked all 65 possible (y=k/64) values and ten current lambdas; maximum relative difference from direct chord ratio was <5e-16.

# Agent 17: statistical evidence and opening risk

Claimed 2026-10-15T22:38:47Z with run `453cc0aed51cf34df4248439`, normal mode (random draw 5). Original lease must not be refreshed.

## Completed and pushed

- `scripts/aichi_evidence.py` implements all-placement exact Clopper-Pearson intervals using SciPy, Bonferroni simultaneous coverage, per-rank published/untested distinction, draws as failures, provenance digests, and candidate 99.5%-threshold indicator.
- `tests/test_aichi_evidence.py`: deterministic synthetic tests. Independently ran a nine-test expanded local suite, all passing. GitHub manual CI run 38819416785 succeeded with the shorter published suite and live published-deck ingestion.
- `results/aichi-2026/evidence-protocol.md`, `results/README.md` link, manual-only `.github/workflows/aichi-deck-ingest.yml` test integration, and `communications/broadcast/2026-10-15T224610Z_agent17_field-confidence.md`.

## Statistical finding

29 exact published lists, missing ranks 23/25/28. Under equal-placement weighting and [0,1] unknown-matchup intervals, 29/32 = 90.625% is the maximum field *lower* bound possible from testing the 29 only. For a two-sided Bonferroni 95% joint Clopper-Pearson interval, 1,428 flawless wins per placement (45,696 total) suffice to give lower mean >99.5% if all 32 lists are known and trials are independent. No actual gameplay trials measured. This criterion is intentionally conservative and does not validate engine or policies.

## Additional local analysis (not yet committed)

The champion list has 14 Basics. Radiant Eternatus (1) and Tapu Lele-GX (2) have Bench-play Abilities. Exact probability that an accepted seven-card opening forces starting Active from these identified three = [C(49,7)-C(46,7)]/[C(60,7)-C(46,7)] = 9.731780%. Radiant sole-Basic accepted opening = 2.815545%. Compare hypothetical +1 fourth Shadow Rider V: 8.288494% forced and 11.750079% mulligans, vs baseline 13.859068%. Verified with 500,000 multivariate-hypergeometric samples, seed 20261015. This is opening opportunity cost only; no win effect. Attempts to create result file were blocked by connector safety checks; retry later.

## Next work

1. Ensure exact 29-opponent list, card-print IDs and legality before any stats claim.
2. Integrate evaluator directly with match-result driver once Agent 2 finishes it; audit seed independence, stopping policy and 32-placement coverage.
3. Quantify opening-prize joint effects and identify impactful structural improvements, validated against executable game state.

### Rank-3 sequencing refinement — Eri vs Energy denial, cross-field Arven timing

- Corrected the Snorlax report's disruption language after checking exact card text: Eri TEF146 discards up to two Item cards from the opponent's hand (so it can discard a held Field Blower); Plumeria BUS120 discards an Energy from an opponent's Pokémon after its controller discards two of their own cards; Team Flare Grunt XY129 discards an Energy from the opponent's Active. Plumeria does **not** disrupt the opponent's Item/Tool hand. Keep Field Blower in deck until Arven searches it. Paid VMAX retreat is exposed to Energy denial; Latias ex only grants free retreat to Basic Pokémon.
- Cross-field Arven timing: rank15 AOR3 locks Item cards only, not Pokémon Tools; Stealthy Hood can be attached under the lock, and Arven remains playable as a Supporter to fetch Hood + Field Blower (Field Blower remains blocked). Pre-lock timing matters only if Field Blower is needed to remove an active Stadium/opposing Hoods. Against rank3 Snorlax Block, no persistent Item lock exists, so preserve Arven until Snorlax + Tool Jammer actually traps the Active; VS Seeker can still recover Arven later.

### Rank-15 turn-order correction — 17 November 10:05 UTC

- I initially wrote that rank15 could establish Vileplume AOR3 before the candidate's first turn when the candidate went second. That was wrong and is superseded in the report, README, and Agent50 handoff.
- Exact rank15 list has Grand Tree ×1, Technical Machine: Evolution ×2, Rare Candy ×4, Oddish ×2, Gloom ×2, Vileplume AOR3 ×2. If the opponent goes first, first-turn evolution restrictions (and Grand Tree's own explicit restriction) prevent AOR3 on that first turn. If the opponent goes second, Technical Machine: Evolution may evolve Oddish to Gloom on its first turn, but it advances only one Stage per chosen Pokémon; the candidate has already acted, and Vileplume Stage 2 can be established only on a later turn. The Advanced Player's Manual C-12 says evolution effects can evolve on a first turn unless specified otherwise; the general rule prevents a Pokémon that just evolved from evolving again that turn.
- Correct conclusion: the candidate has at least one turn before AOR3 Stage 2 can be established in either seat order, but Hood itself can still be attached under AOR3 Item lock because it is a Pokémon Tool. Arven can search Hood + Field Blower during the lock; only the Field Blower Item is unplayable. The pre-lock window matters only if Field Blower must remove a Stadium/opposing Tools. Shadow Rider VMAX Max Geist remains a non-Basic attack route under BUS6.
- Corrected report commit `05f6740b738f404fde831471baf3b9984865bc09`; decision-board correction `538c98ad9f2164f221ae855ad4fc96d2c50fe960`; Agent50 correction handoff `5aa745e5a5b6a238bdf819e173cfb6a3607bcf4a`. The earlier incorrect Agent50 message `43cdf1486fad553cfd259f133274e71389ee9d5b` is superseded.

Megaton Blower strips all attached Tools; Field Blower removes up to two; Faba can be recycled. Candidate Arven+Field Blower+Hood handles the immediate Snorlax/Tool Jammer trap but not a durable Hood lock. Acerola bypasses Block if the Active is damaged. Wobbuffet Bide Barricade exempts Psychic Pokémon, including Shadow Rider VMAX and Latias ex; rank7 Mimikyu Safeguard blocks V/ex attacks, so candidate needs GX/non-V/non-ex attackers.

Manual B-02 says pre-Sword & Shield Pokémon Tools remain a separate Trainer type; C-19 says Item-only locks affect the player and do not block Pokémon Tools. Thus Vileplume AOR3's Item-only lock does **not** prevent attaching Hood or Float Stone. Arven remains playable as a Supporter and can search Hood + Field Blower during the lock: attach Hood; Field Blower (Item) remains unplayable. Pre-lock timing matters only if Field Blower is needed to remove Path to the Peak/Silent Lab or opposing Hoods, not for Hood attachment. ## 17 November 2026 14:53 UTC — rank-1 Unfair Stamp response caveat

- While reviewing the rank-1 first-turn-KO branch, found one additional source-grounded response omitted from both current access enumerators: rank1's published 60 includes **Unfair Stamp ×1** (sv6-165; `resources/cards/en/sv6.json`). Its condition is live after the candidate KOs one of rank1's Pokémon on the candidate's preceding turn. It shuffles both hands, then rank1 draws five and the candidate draws two. As an Item it can be played with other Items and one Supporter in the same turn.
- This may disrupt a candidate-held reserve Hood or VS Seeker while rank1 assembles Field Blower/Teammates, but it also disrupts rank1's own hand and gives the candidate two replacement cards. No net access effect is known. Do not treat this as a guaranteed negative or as a reason to avoid all favorable first-turn KOs. Agent43's reserve enumerator and Agent35's BC/VS response-access enumerator do not model this branch.

## 17 November 2026 14:56 UTC — Teammates can fetch Field Blower + Unfair Stamp together

- Followed the Unfair Stamp caveat through the actual rank-1 Teammates effect. Local card-pool text confirms `xy5-141` searches for **up to two cards**; rank1's published 60 contains Teammates ×1, Field Blower ×1 and Unfair Stamp ×1. In the first-turn-KO scenario, both Teammates and Unfair Stamp are live. If Field Blower and Unfair Stamp are both in deck, Teammates can fetch both, then rank1 can play Field Blower to remove Hood and Unfair Stamp afterward to shuffle both hands. If Stamp is already in hand, Teammates can fetch Field Blower. BC → VS Seeker → Teammates can enable the same Supporter line when Teammates was not naturally seen.
- This combined sequence requires Unfair Stamp to be unspent and available (not Prized/discarded). It may disrupt the candidate's reserve Hood/VS Seeker, but it also shuffles rank1's hand and gives the candidate two cards; no net effect or probability has been quantified. The current reserve and BC/VS access enumerators do not model it.
- Expanded the Unfair Stamp caveat in the Agent17 synthesis to identify the exact Teammates → Field Blower + Unfair Stamp sequence: `results/aichi-2026/2026-11/agent17-two-hood-deployment-rule-20261117.md`, commit **c7804f28f4bbd885805fc4a8a2862d2f2dc6f58e**, verified blob **0fb3d71c878e5ba160b906c10536211b8883ebeb**.

### 2026-11-25 21:53 UTC — GO_FIRST chronology correction (supersedes 21:49 note)

- My 21:49 setup-scope summary repeated an incorrect "GO_FIRST after setup" chronology from Agent4/Agent46. Read the pinned Twinleaf `setup-reducer.ts` at `a9905a76a6c4103ba4b6cb9a49e86bad795c5c05`: standard `setupGame` resolves `WhoBeginsEffect`, then `CoinFlipPrompt`, then immediately sends `ConfirmPrompt:GO_FIRST` to the coin-flip winner; only after that do both players draw 7, mulligan, and choose starting Pokémon / Bench. The alternative setup path also decides who begins before draw/setup.
- Therefore GO_FIRST cannot use opening hand, mulligan, starting Basic, Active/Bench, or opponent-starter information. The actor receiving the prompt is the coin-flip winner; the prompt itself conveys that fact. The V3 projection only exposes the actor-owned engine `GO_FIRST` prompt, so no separate coin-flip/winner field is required for that decision. Starting Basic / optional Bench selection are later, distinct decisions with hand/setup information; `WANT_TO_DRAW_CARDS` is the optional post-opponent-mulligan bonus draw.
- Sent corrections to Agent31, Agent4 and Agent46 at `communications/agent31/2026-11/20261125T215300066Z_agent17-go-first-chronology-correction.md`, `communications/agent4/2026-11/20261125T215300066Z_agent17-go-first-chronology-correction.md`, and `communications/agent46/2026-11/20261125T215300066Z_agent17-go-first-chronology-correction.md`. ## 2026-11-25T23:53Z — official partial-draw rule confirmed

- The official Pokémon TCG rulebook PDF resolves the paper partial-draw question: when an effect requests more cards than remain in the deck, draw the remaining cards and continue; the deck-out loss check occurs when the player cannot draw at the beginning of their turn. Source URL is recorded in the rank-3 dependency manifest: `https://assets.pokemon.com/assets/cms2/pdf/trading-card-game/rulebook/par_rulebook_en.pdf`.
- With one card remaining, Player's Ceremony draws that one card; if the player chooses the optional effect, the turn-ending clause then applies. The remaining strategic question is whether an opponent with 1–2 cards in deck declines the shared effect; this is not modeled in v1.2 and remains untested.
